# Tankbook – Completed & Cut Tasks

*Every closed row - ticked `[x]` or decided against `[cut]` - in the section it was filed under – split out of `docs/TASKS.md` (2026-09-08)
so the open backlog can be read without carrying 274 finished ones. The index in `docs/TASKS.md`
says which file any task id is in. These rows are still the authority for a shipped decision and
its reasoning: a row that is reopened moves back to `TASKS.md` rather than being rewritten here, and
re-proposing a cut row needs the recorded reason to have changed (`TASKS.md` -> Status legend).*

*`PHASES.md` broken into agent-sized tasks. Each task names its deliverable and the **checks that make it done** – a task without its checks green is not done, and checks land in the same PR as the code (TESTING.md levels: L1 unit · L2 contract · L3 sync-scenario · L4 UI/snapshot · L5 accuracy · M manual). Task IDs are stable – reference them in branches/PRs (`p0-4-grdb-migrations`).*

## Implied in every task's checks: it builds and it lints

The per-task checks below are **in addition to** the baseline gate, which is never restated per row because it applies to all of them: every tier a task touches must compile and its linter must exit **0** (`CLAUDE.md` hard rule 14, `docs/TESTING.md` → the baseline gate). Run `swiftlint lint` **from the repo root**, and judge it by exit code. A task whose feature tests pass but which leaves the build broken or the linter red is **not** done and does not get an `[x]`.

## Status legend

`[x]` done and independently verified – its own checks **and** the baseline gate re-run outside the implementing agent · `[~]` partially landed, gap named · `[ ]` not started · `[!]` **blocked on an action only the product owner can take** (a purchase, a legal decision, an account) - not work an agent can pick up · `[cut]` **decided against, with the reason recorded in the row and in the owning doc** – a cut task is not work waiting to be picked up, and re-proposing it needs the recorded reason to have changed. Status is recorded here so a fresh session knows what to pick up without re-deriving it.

## Version scope

*`CLAUDE.md` → Version scope is the convention. **A row without a marker is v1** – the launch build owes it, and the launch triage below says in which tier. Rows marked **[v1.0.x]**, **[v1.1]**, **[v1.x]** or **[v2]** are deferred to that release; the marker repeats the triage's target so a row can be read on its own. Section AG is v2 in its entirety.*

## Launch triage (2026-08-29) – what v1 needs, what waits

*Every open row (P-, W-, PR-, PJ-) sorted against one question: **can the app be submitted and
used by a stranger without this?** Three tiers. **Blocker** – submission is impossible or the
first session breaks a hard rule visibly. **Required** – a v1 promise (`VISION.md` MVP row, a
journey "always/never", or a written security/privacy commitment) that the code does not keep;
ship without it only with a written product-owner exception. **Deferred** – real, tracked, and
not a v1 condition; target named. Sizes are relative (S under a day of agent work, M a few days,
L a week-plus). Ordering inside a tier is the recommended sequence.*

**Two decisions this triage assumes, each reversible by the product owner:**

1. **The backend ships with v1.** `api.tankbook.live` is deliberately 502 today. Without it the
   app loses not only sync and cloud extract but **import parsing** (server-side by rule 9),
   **fresh exchange rates** (the seed pack ages from day one), remote config and the `appUpdate`
   notice – three MVP rows and the only way to retire a bad build. The backend is built and
   L2/L3-tested (P4, P5 complete); what is missing is the deployment itself, which no row tracks.
2. **v1 ships what is built, not only VISION's MVP column.** Service, reminders and sync are
   "v1.x" in `VISION.md` but P3 and P4 are complete; they ship, so their unreachable or
   half-wired parts count as required. EV charging (J6) and the Pro tier stay out.

### The v1.1 priority queue (product owner, 2026-08-31)

Eleven rows pulled out of the v1.1 bucket and **ordered**. Version markers are unchanged - these
stay `[v1.1]`/`[v1.x]` - this is a sequencing decision, not a scope change. Rows are marked
**PRIORITY** in place.

**Why these eleven.** Seven are the J7 cluster: P3 built the nouns (service entries, parts, tire
sets, reminders) and skipped most of the verbs that connect them, which is how the phase reads
COMPLETE while the journey does not hold together. Four are already-built machinery that nothing
calls - the cheapest rows in the backlog.

| # | Task | What the user hits today |
|---|---|---|

Rows 8-11 are the pattern worth watching: **the mechanism exists, tested, and no user can reach
it.** Ask what produces a case, not only what handles it - the same question that produced P6.20.

### SH · Ship tasks (no row covered these)

| ID | Task | Checks (done when...) |
|---|---|---|
| **[x]** SH.4 | **DONE 2026-09-01, verified.** Google is **wired, without the SDK** (product owner: a third-party SDK and its transitive dependencies are not worth one button). OAuth 2.0 authorization-code + PKCE through `ASWebAuthenticationSession`: `GoogleOAuth` (core, pure, 25 L1 tests) builds the request, validates the callback and shapes the exchange; `GoogleWebAuthenticator` (app) presents the browser and runs the POST on a bare `URLSession` - NOT `TankbookHTTPClient`, which rightly refuses `oauth2.googleapis.com` and must carry no Tankbook bearer. **The dead control is gone by construction**: the button renders exactly when `GoogleOAuth.Configuration.provisioned` returns non-nil, so a build with no client id offers Apple alone - measured, a Release build ships `GoogleClientID` empty. The J11a wrong-provider question is gated the same way. Mutation-checked: the `code_challenge` reverted to the verifier, and the `state` check relaxed to presence-only, each fail their test. **It also found and fixed a live account-takeover vector** - see the new `PR.35` row. Commit at merge | L4 `SignInUITests` 7/7 on `iPhone 17`; unit 1152; screenshots `SH.4-sign-in-google-{en,ru}.png` opened in EN and RU |
| **[x]** PR.35 | **DONE 2026-09-01, verified. Found while wiring SH.4.** `AppleGoogleIdTokenVerifier` verified an identity token's **signature** and never its `aud` or `iss` - and Apple's and Google's JWKS sign tokens for *every* client on their platforms. So any developer shipping an app with Apple or Google sign-in could collect their own users' id tokens and replay them at `POST /auth/session` to take over the matching Tankbook account. Live for Apple today, independent of Google. Now checked per provider against `Auth:AppleAudiences` / `Auth:GoogleAudiences` before any other claim is read, and it **fails closed**: an unconfigured allowlist refuses every token rather than accepting any. `Auth:Audience` is a different thing (the audience this server *stamps*) and `docs/STORE.md` §4.3 said to set it for this purpose - that sentence is corrected. **The class had no tests at all**: every L2 endpoint test injects `TestIdTokenSigner.Verifier`, a reimplementation that checks the signature and the expiry and nothing else. **Blocks `SH.1`** - the audiences must be set at deploy or every sign-in refuses | 14 new L2 tests against the REAL verifier; mutation-checked twice (fail-open on an empty allowlist, and dropping the per-provider split) each failing exactly its own test; backend 295, `dotnet format` 0 |

| **[x]** P2.3b | **DONE 2026-08-30, verified (superseded same day by P2.3c).** The row shipped as a LIMIT; the product owner corrected it - a car takes 92/95/100 interchangeably, so fuelKinds suggests and never limits. Commits 25b0283 + d7ec242. Originally: The Fuel row offers fuels the car cannot burn.** Confirm shows a `95 / Diesel` chooser because the seed car is `fuelKinds: [.petrol95, .diesel]` - a car that burns both, which does not exist. `docs/DESIGN.md` already makes fuel kind **conditional for display** ("a diesel-only car printing Diesel on every row is noise dressed as information"); the same rule must govern **input**. Petrol grades (92/95/98/100) share a tank and are a real driver's choice; **LPG/CNG/E85 alongside petrol are real** (bi-fuel and flex-fuel); **diesel with petrol is not**. So: the row offers exactly `vehicle.fuelKinds`, and with a single kind it is a static value rather than a chooser - while staying correctable, because hard rule 13 forbids a value the user cannot change. Note `fuelGrade` is separate and still varies for a diesel car (the corpus has `ДТ-Е-К5 Танеко` and `АТ-Л-К5 Ультра`) | L1: a diesel-only car is never offered petrol, and vice versa; a petrol car with 92+95 is offered exactly those two; a single-kind car renders no chooser yet the value is still editable; AddVehicle rejects diesel+petrol while allowing petrol+LPG/CNG/E85; the seeds stop describing impossible cars |

L4: the section renders only when lines are detected and the sheet stays the ordinary form otherwise. **L5: pending more mixed fixtures** - the corpus now has TWO (`receipt-009` fuel+water, `receipt-025` fuel+service fee), which is enough to exercise the detector against a second shape but still not enough for a percentage. `receipt-025` is now classified by the fiscal cross-check (its QR grand total exceeds the fuel line), but its detection by **line structure** - the path that works on the 20 of 32 receipts with no usable QR - is still unasserted, and that is what this gate needs. **A THIRD mixed fixture landed 2026-08-26**: `screenshots/screenshot-008-circlek-jugla-lv-mixed.png` (Circle K Jugla, Rīga) - eight lines, the fuel line **third**, Latvian item names, and it **reconciles completely** (fuel 112.63 + non-fuel 10.36 = 122.99 total; fuel discount 2.39 + shop discount 0.78 = the printed 3.17). It is the corpus's first mixed document that is not a photograph and not in Russian, and the parser currently returns the **grand total 122.99** for it - a live hard-rule-4 violation this gate exists to catch. Its discriminator is the unit token, `L` against Latvian `Gab.` (pieces), which the vocabulary did not have. |

| **[x]** P2.14 | **`FuelExtractor.modal` is non-deterministic, and it destabilises every accuracy number in the project.** The total-finder resolves ties with `Dictionary(grouping:)`, whose iteration order Swift does not guarantee, and then `modes.max(by:)` on equal `primaryCounts` returns whichever element that order left last - with `?? modes.first` order-dependent too. So when two total candidates tie on count **and** on primary-label count, the answer is whatever the hash seed decided. Found by P4.13, which required three sweeps per image and watched the receipt score flip between **29/96 and 30/96** across identical runs (`receipt-021`, `-026`, `-029`). **This is not only a parser bug: `high-water.json` and `PumpPhotoGate.measuredHits` are gates asserted against a live score, so a tie-break that moves makes CI itself intermittently red - and it means the receipts mark cannot safely be raised from its stale 45/93 to the live 46/96 until this lands.** The fix must be deterministic **and the tie-break must be chosen deliberately and stated**, not just stabilised: the strongest candidate is to **return `nil`**, because the parser already refuses rather than guesses elsewhere (a printed `0.00` becomes `nil`, an unmarked operand pair returns `nil`) and hard rule 13 says a confident wrong value is worse than an empty field the user fills. If refusing lowers the score, that is **information, not failure** | L1: a fixture whose totals tie on both counts returns the **same** answer across 100 runs **in separate processes** - one process shares a hash seed and would pass while the bug is live; the chosen tie-break is asserted **by name** (nil, or the stated rule), not merely 'stable'; `receipt-021`/`-026`/`-029` are asserted individually. L5: re-score after the fix and **raise the receipts high-water mark in the same change**, with the new number proven stable across repeated runs. **Vacuous traps**: asserting determinism by calling the function twice in one process (same seed, always agrees); asserting 'a value is returned' rather than which; fixing `modal` alone and leaving `?? modes.first` order-dependent |

| **[cut]** P2.8 | ~~Foundation Models normalization layer (iOS 26+ gated)~~ **Cut 2026-08-25, not deferred.** The on-device model reports itself `unavailable` on an unsupported device language - the shipped iOS 26.5 SDK carries `SystemLanguageModel.UnavailableReason.unsupportedLanguageOrLocale` next to `supportedLanguages` - and **Russian is not in the supported set** (nor Kazakh). The corpus is 32 RU/KZ receipts and receipts score 41.6%, so the layer would have been unavailable exactly where the rules parser is weakest and available only where it already does best. The task's own check (*ships only if it strictly improves on an A/B against the corpus*) is therefore unsatisfiable in principle, not merely unmeasured - a stronger reason to stop than P2.7's 0/30. **Not blocked on the EU**, which has had Apple Intelligence since iOS 18.4 (April 2025); the constraint is language, not jurisdiction. Reasoning and the re-open condition (Apple adding Russian to `supportedLanguages`) live in `docs/VISION.md` -> "Why tier 2 was cut". Normalization for Russian receipts is now tier 3's job (the cloud LLM gateway) | **No code shipped, by decision.** The measurement that would have gated it cannot be taken |

The app then draws the bar through `safeAreaInset(edge: .bottom)`, which adds the real inset (~34 pt on iPhone 17) underneath it, leaving **~62 pt of dead space below the labels against the artboard's 28**. Spend the artboard's allowance **through** the safe area instead: `max(8, 28 - safeAreaInsets.bottom)` - ~42 pt on an indicator device, exactly 28 on an SE, and the 8 pt floor stops the labels touching the indicator on tall devices (the opposite mistake). `contentHeight` is derived from this padding, so `TabRoots.toastBottomClearance` and `contentBottomClearance` (used by `HomeView` and `TrendsView` to clear the raised capture circle) move with it. **General lesson worth carrying: an artboard's bottom padding is never a safe-area inset - porting it verbatim double-counts every time.** | L4: the existing "last row clears the tab bar" assertion still holds; a **device without a home indicator renders the artboard's exact 28 pt** and one with an indicator renders the floor, asserted against injected insets rather than a runtime measurement; screenshots in **both themes** (the defect was reported on the light shell) and EN+RU |

One gate: `AppStore.resetForTestsOncePerLaunch()` performs the wipe at most once per process; all five call sites (Home, RecentlyDeleted, TankLevel, TireSet, Reminder seeds) use it; the three per-type flags are gone. (2) The capture script's `en` branch passed no language arguments, so a preceding `ru` launch's `-AppleLanguages "(ru)"` persisted and `en` captures rendered Russian (P1.4-home, P3.2-parts-shelf). The `en` branch now passes `-AppleLanguages "(en)" -AppleLocale en_US` explicitly. Re-captured; the two known-bad files verified by size and re-run. | Seeds intact (no seed weakened or deleted, idempotence preserved); `swift test` 0 at 529+; `xcodebuild test` 0 at 108; `swiftlint` 0; EN captures no longer follow RU into Russian |

After the P2.8 cut this is the **only** model-assisted extraction path in the product, and the only one available to Russian receipts. Server-side provider key (hard rule 11 - an IPA is a zip), per-user quota from `llm_usage` returning the documented `402`/`429`, the 4 MB envelope cap, images processed **transiently and never written to disk or blob storage**, and a response that is exactly `ExtractionMeta` fields with per-field confidence. | **[x]** P4.11 | **`Date` does not round-trip exactly through persistence.** `Records.swift` stores every date as `timeIntervalSince1970`; adding the 978307200 s epoch offset shifts the fractional bits out of the Double mantissa, so a record read straight back after a write differs from what was written by **one ulp (~1.2e-7 s)** on `createdAt` / `updatedAt`. Found by P3.1a's round-trip test - the first test in the repo to assert **whole-record** equality rather than field-by-field, which is why it went unnoticed through P0.4. Measured severity: the drift is **idempotent** (a second round trip is stable), so sync's `updatedAt` LWW ordering is not at risk today - but the invariant "a record read back equals the record written" is one that sync, backup round-trips and the S1-S8 scenarios all lean on, and it is currently false. Fix is to store `timeIntervalSinceReferenceDate` (an exact Double round trip), which is a **column format change and needs a migration** - hence a task, not a patch | L1: a record of every entity type read straight back after a write equals what was written, whole-record and with no normalization; the existing sub-millisecond tolerance in `ServiceEntryTests` is **deleted** rather than loosened; migration applies forward over a seeded database and the migrated values are equal to the originals to the second |
| **[x]** P4.12 | **Corpus A/B against the cloud vision model, before P4.10 is designed.** Run 2026-08-26, full sweep (61 images, 0 errors), scored LLM-vs-rules with the one shared `CorpusScorer` and tolerance. **Result per class (rules -> LLM): receipts 46/96 -> 84/96, pump 1/46 -> 31/46, fiscal 1/3 -> 2/3, screenshots 7/24 -> 22/24.** Median latency 6.5-8.3 s, all above the 3 s device budget. The two silent failure modes are committed and asserted as named cases: `receipt-035` is the volume/price swap (70.44 read as litres) and `pump-009` is the decimal-separator shift (40.00 read as 400.0) - **both pass the arithmetic cross-check**. The probe's shift on `pump-005` did not reproduce: that fixture read exactly, three runs in a row, and `pump-009` flipped between correct and shifted across runs - the model is stochastic. **Recommendation (docs/EXTRACTION.md): the gateway cross-checks and suggests, never trusts** - a confident swap is worse than the parser's nil (hard rule 13), and the cross-check is scale- and swap-blind | L5: every class scored LLM-vs-rules with the same scorer and tolerance (offline, against committed `vision-ab/*.json`, no model call in CI); latency per image; the swap and decimal-shift cases asserted explicitly as known failure modes; a written recommendation in `docs/EXTRACTION.md`. `high-water.json` unchanged |
| **[x]** P4.13 | **PaddleOCR as a third extraction arm: embed it in Docker, measure it against the other two.** P4.12 measured the cloud VLM (receipts 84/96, pump 31/46, screenshots 22/24) but found its failures **silent** (swaps and scale shifts that pass the arithmetic cross-check), its output **stochastic**, and **every class median latency above the normative 3 s device budget** (pump peaks at 40 s). PaddleOCR is worth measuring because it attacks three of those four at once: self-hosted, so **no per-call cost and no third-party image sharing**, and plausibly far faster. **Russian is supported** (`cyrillic_PP-OCRv5_mobile_rec`; PaddleOCR-VL covers 109 languages) - the gap that killed the Foundation Models tier. Runs as a **container** (plain `docker run`, never compose) with ASP.NET reaching it behind an injected seam, exactly like `IBlobStorage` / `IApnsClient` / `IRateFeed`. **Two arms, because together they answer what neither answers alone**: **(A)** PP-OCRv5 Cyrillic recognition -> the EXISTING `FuelExtractor`, isolating recognition - if this scores about the same as Vision+parser it **confirms** `docs/EXTRACTION.md`'s central claim that interpretation is the bottleneck; if it scores far better, **that claim is wrong and the doc must be corrected in the same change**; **(B)** PaddleOCR-VL -> fields directly, competing with the DeepSeek arm on accuracy, latency, cost and determinism. Gateway-only: hard rule 1 means this can never be the primary path - on-device Vision stays tier 0. **Measured 2026-08-26:** Arm A (server det + `cyrillic_PP-OCRv5_mobile_rec`) -> `FuelExtractor` scored receipts **29/96** (rules 46/96, DeepSeek 84/96), pump **2/46** (1/46, 31/46), fiscal **1/3** (1/3, 2/3), screenshots **7/24** (7/24, 22/24) - **not** far better, so the interpretation-not-recognition split is **not falsified**, but the real finding is that the parser is **coupled to Vision's output**: PaddleOCR's detector merges `1,869 EUR/L` into one line (Vision splits it), and the Cyrillic-only reader misreads `ИТОГ` as `НТОГ`. PaddleOCR's OCR is **byte-identical across 3 runs**, but the sweep exposed a **pre-existing parser** non-determinism (`FuelExtractor.modal` ties two total candidates and breaks the tie via `Dictionary(grouping:)` iteration order) that flips `receipt-021`/`-026`/`-029` between runs (receipt score 29-30/96). Median latency 4.5-8.4 s, max 21.0 s - **above the 3 s budget in every class** even self-hosted. **Arm B (PaddleOCR-VL-0.9B) is blocked**: `paddlex` calls `is_bfloat16_supported()` with no place argument (raises on paddlepaddle 3.2.2 aarch64); forcing float32 OOM-kills the 7.6 GB Docker VM (exit 137). **Conclusion (docs/EXTRACTION.md): the ops cost is not justified** - Arm A is worse than on-device Vision, self-hosting buys no latency back without a GPU, and Arm B cannot run on modest hardware; PaddleOCR does not earn the fallback slot | L5: both arms scored with the **existing `CorpusScorer` at the same tolerance** (P4.12) - a second, subtly different comparison invalidates everything; per-class hits/total beside the rules and DeepSeek arms; **latency median and max per class against the 3 s budget**; **determinism measured, not assumed** - every image run 3x with variance reported, because the DeepSeek arm flipped `pump-009` between correct and shifted on re-run; scoring runs **offline against committed result files** so CI never needs the container; `high-water.json` unchanged. **Vacuous traps**: reporting only aggregate percentages (two silent failures matter more than a higher average); running each image once and calling it deterministic; scoring arm A against a different tolerance than the rules arm it exists to be compared with |

While iOS Low Power Mode is on, the app postpones **opportunistic** work - launch/foreground/timer sync cycles, attachment upload and prefetch, the rate pack refresh, the catalog pack fetch - and changes nothing else. **The load-bearing distinction is background vs user-initiated**: a save, and any sync/restore/export/retry the user actually tapped, must run exactly as before, because a silently cancelled action the user is watching has no next step (hard rule 7) and reads as a hang. Resumes on `NSProcessInfoPowerStateDidChange`, not at next launch. L1: the config fetch is **never awaited by a view** - launch resolves from the held snapshot (live/cache/bundled); the mutation is awaiting the refresh before first draw. **`api.tankbook.live` does not answer yet, and that is intentional** (product owner, 2026-08-28). nginx proxies it and returns
**502** because no backend is listening on the upstream port. Postponed deliberately, not broken: the app is local-first, so an
unreachable API costs sync, cloud extract and import parse and **nothing else** - every screen keeps working offline (hard rule 1).
Two consequences worth knowing while it stays down: the config guardrail's `GET /health` probe cannot promote a candidate
`apiBaseUrl`, so the bundled default stands; and P6.18's update requirement resolves `.none`, because a config document that never
arrives carries no `appUpdate` - which is the fail-open rule behaving exactly as designed.

**Two open product decisions, neither of them engineering:** the analytics choice (none / the CDN
operator's own / a hosted cookieless service - see `SITE.md`), and whether **RU 152-ФЗ** obligations
apply alongside GDPR. W2 ships GDPR-framed with the second question tracked, not guessed at.

**Three Russian copy items** found by reading the rendered artboard, held pending the product owner:
`БЕСПЛАТНО … БЕСПЛАТНЫЙ` repeats a root in one strip; `Топливо, зарядки, сервис и всё остальное
хранится` takes a singular verb on a plural list; `как удобно` reads clipped against `как вам удобно`.

## PJ · Journeys review (2026-08-29)

*Source: `docs/JOURNEYS.md` reviewed stage by stage against the tree at `93d2619` by four
read-only passes (brief `agents/briefs/REVIEW-JOURNEYS.md`); every bug-level claim below was
re-verified by hand before it was written. Gaps already tracked elsewhere are cited, not re-filed:
PR.2 (sign-out revoke), PR.7 (auto-retry), PR.13 (offline vs server-down), PR.14 (S1 row + S7
toast - its deliverable should also name Recently deleted's "Overwritten by sync" section and
Compare, both fixtures), PR.22 (LLM quota note), PR.30 (reminder hours), P2.6/P2.7/P5.5b/P6.9
open halves. J6 (EV charge) and J12 (second driver) are v1.x/v2 per `VISION.md` and are not
filed, except for the one live defect J6 leaves in v1 (PJ.12).*

*Five ticked rows claim more than the code does; PJ.47 footnotes them: **P1.1** (Welcome root
and its three edges were never built), **P2.2/P2.3** (parser ported, sheet takes a prefill - the
seam camera -> OCR -> prefill -> sheet was never a task), **P3.4** (Reminders has no production
entry point), **P4.4** (wrong-provider detection exists, the entry that arms it does not),
**P5.2** (S8 backfill runs only under a DEBUG flag), **P6.3** (only the timeout branch of F4
renders).*

*The shape of the finding, in one sentence: the Confirm sheet, the engines and the import review
are built and L4-covered, but **the scan door is painted on** - no camera or Photos image ever
becomes a `ConfirmPrefill` outside launch-argument seeds - and four screens that the journeys
route through (Welcome, Reminders, the S5 notice, the post-sign-in push) exist only behind
`-force*` fixtures.*

## RV · Reviewer bugs (first TestFlight build, 2026-09-02/03)

*Found by running build **1.0.0+466** on a real device against the live backend, and by reading
production logs. Every row here is something no test caught, because each sits in a seam the suites
do not span: a client/server contract, a UIKit lifetime, a tier default, or a screen nobody drove on
hardware. `RV.1`-`RV.5`, `RV.7`-`RV.9` and `RV.11`-`RV.16` are fixed; `RV.10`, `RV.17`-`RV.20`, `RV.22`-`RV.26` and `RV.28`-`RV.35` are open.*

| # | Bug | Check |
|---|---|---|
| **[x]** RV.1 | **The rate pack 400'd on every launch.** `RateStore` asked for a **two-year** window; the server caps at `Rates:MaxPackDays` **400**, so every refresh was rejected - five in one session, and it would have been every launch forever. A number in two places that disagreed (`PRACTICES.md`). Invisible because the client tests use a `RateFetcher` double that accepts any range and the server tests choose their own: **both sides tested, the contract between them not** | The client's requested span is recorded and compared against the server cap; mutation-checked - restoring the two-year window fails with `730 <= 400` |
| **[x]** RV.2 | **Choosing a photo from the library did nothing** - the app stayed on the capture screen. `PHPickerViewControllerDelegate` set `isPresented = false` first, which tears down the representable and releases the `Coordinator`, so the `[weak self]` in the async `loadObject` callback was already nil and the pick was dropped. A **race**, which is why review and the simulator missed it: a small local image can beat the teardown, an iCloud photo that must download never does | `self` is captured strongly (a `@MainActor` class is `Sendable`), so delivery no longer depends on how long the load takes. Needs a device re-test with an iCloud photo |
| **[x]** RV.3 | **The per-request log line buried everything else.** One `http.request` at Information per call, several per screen, hiding `auth.session`, `sync.push`, `blob.commit` | Success is `Debug`, 4xx `Information`, 5xx `Warning`, plus a readable summary. Re-levelled, not removed: a test pins that the successful line still appears at `Debug` |
| **[x]** RV.4 | **DONE 2026-09-03, product owner: the free tier gets cloud extract in v1.** The allowance was 0, so `POST /extract` answered 402 to every user - correct against `API.md`'s "LLM gateway (Pro)", except Pro is cut from v1 (`P6.16`), so the gate applied to nobody and refused everyone while the served config document promised `llmQuota.cloudFallback: 50`. Both halves were individually right and the pair was a lie. Free now gets exactly the advertised 50/day, from a named constant, and it stays configuration (`LlmGateway__TierRequestsPerPeriod__free`) so the number can move without a deploy. The alternative was capture on on-device Vision alone - **38.3%** on receipts against the cloud model's 84/96 (`P4.12`) | 3 L1 tests: free gets the advertised number, free is entitled **at all** (asserted separately - an equality check against a zero constant would still pass at zero), and an unknown tier is still refused. Mutation-checked: restoring 0 fails two of the three |
| **[x]** RV.5 | **DONE 2026-09-03. No preview or confirmation after a capture.** A shot is taken and the flow moves on with nothing shown; the user cannot see what was captured, accept it, or re-take. Requested by the product owner from the device walk. Touches `JOURNEYS.md` (J1/J3), `SCREENMAP.md` (a screen between capture and Confirm) and `ERRORS.md` (the re-take affordance), so the docs move with it - and hard rule 15 applies: re-take and "type it instead" are peers, not a failure branch | DONE: `CaptureReviewView` is a full-screen cover between both capture doors and the Confirm sheet - the image fitted (never cropped), **Use this** (which is where the recognition pipeline now runs, so a re-take costs no OCR), **Re-take** (back to the live camera, nothing kept) and **Type it** as its equal-width neighbour. `CaptureReviewUITests` (4 tests, run): the shot is shown and both verdicts are reachable, Type it is a peer by frame geometry and opens the manual form, Use this reaches Confirm with the pipeline's real litres, and Re-take returns to the camera and **does not** present Confirm. `JOURNEYS.md` (J1, J3, J3b), `SCREENMAP.md` (graph, inventory, dead-end audit, a section for the step) and `ERRORS.md` (Capture) moved with it; screenshots EN and RU |
| **[x]** RV.6 (2026-09-04, verified by orchestrator: swift build 0, `swift test` **1250/117**, localization gate 0, app build 0, zero lint errors outside the sibling worktree; mutation-checked personally - restoring the unconditional refetch fails 3 tests including the round-trip count assertion, restored byte-identical. Commit `48d9313`. **INVESTIGATED 2026-09-04, read-only pro agent. Verdict: PARTIALLY fixed - the automatic burst is gone, a navigation shape remains, and TWO claims in this row's own text are wrong.** **(1) RV.18 fixed the automatic re-poll**, which was the most probable source of the production log: pre-RV.18 every `scenePhase == .active` transition ran a full opportunistic cycle -> `runSync` -> `refresh()` -> a devices fetch, and RV.18 measured launch alone firing **twice ~0.6 s apart**. `runOpportunisticSync` now returns early inside 30 s, so that shape is impossible. **(2) A different shape still reproduces, and it is not a poll at all**: `AppSync.refresh()` re-fetches the device count **unconditionally on every call** (`AppSync.swift:312-315`; `fetchedDeviceCount` is set to nil and re-fetched, never reused), and `SettingsView`'s `.task` calls `refresh()` on **every appearance** - ungated. So one "check my devices" round trip costs **three** fetches: Settings appears (#1), push AccountDevices (#2, `didLoad`-guarded so once per push), pop back and Settings `.task` re-runs (#3). Gear -> account -> back -> account is four real GETs in well under 14 s with no sync cycle involved. **There is no de-duplication anywhere**: no in-flight guard, no minimum interval, and RV.18's 30 s gate covers only `runOpportunisticSync`, not this path. **CORRECTION 1 - this row's premise is factually wrong**: *"the per-IP limits key on it"* - there is **no rate limit on `GET /account/devices`**. `Program.cs:584` maps it with no `.RequireRateLimiting` while six neighbouring groups have one, and `API.md`'s table lists limits only for `auth/session`, `auth/refresh`, `import/parse`, `extract`, `sync/push`, `blobs/begin` and `feedback`. The real cost is battery, plus the future risk if a limit is ever attached to this un-throttled GET. **CORRECTION 2 - RV.22 did NOT worsen it**, which was the orchestrator's stated suspicion when briefing: `SyncStateChip` reads only the computed `surfaceState` plus stored counts, never calls `refresh()` or `devices()`, and is gated to the active tab root. **Also noted**: RV.26's refresher can double a devices GET on a 401 (the screen's client wires it, the count path deliberately does not) - real, but not the polling shape.**) | **`GET /v1/account/devices` is polled repeatedly** - four calls in 14 seconds (21:07:23, :28, :30, :37), then more. Looks like a screen refetching on every appearance rather than on change. Cheap per call, but it is the shape that becomes a battery and rate-limit problem, and the per-IP limits key on it **ACCEPTANCE CORRECTED 2026-09-04 by the investigation**: "fetch on appear and on an explicit refresh" is what `AccountDevicesView` **already does** (`didLoad`-guarded, one fetch per push) - so the original acceptance would have passed against the bug. The residual repetition is the **sync surface's count fetch piggybacking on every `refresh()`**, which the row never named. The fix is there: either reuse `fetchedDeviceCount` and re-fetch only when nil (clearing it explicitly on sign-in, sign-out and after a revoke/delete), or drop the count fetch from `refresh()` entirely and let the AccountDevices screen be the sole fetcher - collapsing the round trip from three fetches to one. **The test must assert a REQUEST COUNT over a recording transport** - `GET /v1/account/devices` fires <= 1 across `Settings -> AccountDevices -> pop -> AccountDevices` - never "the screen loaded", which passes against the bug. The seeded `AccountStubTransport`/`SeededLaunchTransport` already serve this path, so the assertion is cheap | The device list is fetched on appear and on an explicit refresh, not on every re-render; L4 asserts the call count across a navigation cycle |
| **[x]** RV.7 | **DONE 2026-09-03.** `RatesHostedService` now runs one pass BEFORE entering the timer loop. `PeriodicTimer.WaitForNextTickAsync` waits a full interval before its first tick, so with the 6-hour default there was no pass for six hours after a start - and every deploy restarts the process and resets that clock, so deploying more often than six-hourly meant it **never ran**. That is what production showed: `/v1/rates` served a correctly-shaped, permanently empty pack while answering 200, so nothing looked wrong. The pass is idempotent - the job's own comment calls the interval "a liveness knob, not a correctness one" - so one extra run at boot costs a query and removes the dependence on uptime | L2 drives `StartAsync` with a 24-hour interval, so no tick can arrive, and asserts the feed is reached. **The first version of this test was VACUOUS** - it called `RunOnePassAsync` directly, so deleting the startup call from `ExecuteAsync` still passed; it now drives the real entry point and the mutation fails it |
| **[x]** RV.8 | **DONE 2026-09-03. A ten-second wait with nothing on screen.** Production measured `llm.extract DurationMs=10218` on a real receipt; the Confirm sheet showed **nothing at all** for the first 3 s and a **motionless hourglass** for the remaining 7, which reads as a finished statement rather than as work in progress. The gateway session already knew - `phase` has carried `.running` since P6.3 - but only `.budgetExpired` was ever rendered, so the state that covers the whole wait was the one state with no view. L4: each state renders its own chip, the chip is hittable, and its tap destination differs by state (sign-in vs Settings vs the conflict surface). **Colour is never the only channel** (`docs/DESIGN.md` accessibility floor), so every state carries a distinct GLYPH and an accessibility label naming the state - assert the labels, not the hues, since XCUITest cannot read colour and that is exactly how P1.1 shipped a rule-5 violation green. The in-flight state uses the system `ProgressView` and degrades under Reduce Motion (the RV.8 precedent). Screenshots EN and RU of every state, dark theme - RU is where a state label overflows. Hard rule 12: the chip may log counts and state names, never a domain value  **WAITING SETTLED 2026-09-03 (product owner): the ARROW, not the queue count.** So Waiting is `icloud.and.arrow.up` and the count stays in the label rather than on the glyph - one mark per state, and the chip does not have to re-layout as the queue changes. **One decision still open**: whether to add the `ok` green token for Synced (proposed `#4FD18C` dark / `#0E7A46` light, both unverified against `PaletteAccentGuardTests`) or fall back to `headlight` for Synced and `inkSoft` for Waiting and add no token at all. Everything else in the design is settled and this task is otherwise ready to brief - it remains blocked only on RV.21 landing, since the gear it sits beside exists on one tab of three today.  **GREEN APPROVED 2026-09-03 (product owner): add the `ok` token, `#4FD18C` dark / `#0E7A46` light.** So the design is fully settled - `icloud.slash`/inkSoft, `exclamationmark.icloud`/warn, spinner/headlight, `icloud.and.arrow.up`/headlight, `checkmark.icloud`/**ok**. RV.21 has landed, so nothing blocks this any more. **The values are proposals until measured**: both must clear `PaletteAccentGuardTests` at 4.5:1 on `midnight` and `dash` in BOTH themes before they ship, and the token is added to `design/tokens.json` (which generates `Theme.generated.swift`) and to `docs/DESIGN.md`, never as a literal in a view. A green that fails the guard is a rule-5 violation, and the guard is the only thing that catches it - XCUITest cannot read colour. |

The row becomes a real control that opens `UIApplication.openSettingsURLString`. Cheap, native, no relaunch hack, nothing to keep in sync. **Option B - an in-app picker.** Writing `AppleLanguages` into `UserDefaults` does not take effect until the app restarts, because `Bundle.main` resolves its localisation once at launch - so B either shows a "restart to apply" prompt (poor, and users read it as a bug) or routes **every** string through a per-language `Bundle`. Four things that decision pulls in. **(a) The prompt is an error-class surface under hard rule 7 and must name its next step** - "Language changes the next time you open Tankbook", not a bare "restart required". **(b) Never call `exit(0)`.** An app that kills itself to apply a setting is an App Store rejection risk and reads as a crash; the user reopens it. **(c) Do NOT half-apply the change to the running session.** Routing `L10n.localize` (153 call sites) to a new bundle while the 268 `Text(LocalizedStringKey)` sites keep the old one would leave the app in two languages at once - visibly worse than one clean restart. All-or-nothing. **(d) Write nothing at first launch.** No stored preference means "follow the system", so a user who later changes their phone's language sees the app follow; storing the system value at first launch would silently freeze it. The product owner's wording was "default from the system setting of the first launch", and this reading satisfies it while keeping the app in step with the phone - flag it if that was not the intent. (2) `AddVehicleView` saves with `upsertVehicle` and **never calls `noteEntryChanged()`**, so no reload signal is raised for anyone - Garage would not hear it even if it listened. Home escapes the bug only because it observes `carSelection.selectedID`, which the switcher changes when it selects the new car. **Fix both**: a fix that only raises the signal, or only adds the observer, appears to work down one path and not the other | L4: add a car, open Garage **in the same session without relaunching**, and assert the new car is listed - a test that relaunches between the two steps passes against the bug, because a cold start re-runs `.task`. Mutation-checked: removing either half must fail the test  **DONE 2026-09-03, and there were THREE causes, not the two this row diagnosed.** The third would have made a fix for the first two do nothing: `GarageView.load()` opened with `guard !didLoad else { return }`, so every call after the first returned immediately - a reload signal would have arrived and been discarded. Seeding now runs once (`didSeed`) while the **data reloads on every call**. With that, the two known halves landed: Garage gained `.onChange(of: selection.selectedID)` and `.onChange(of: toastCenter.revision)`, mirroring Home and Trends, and `AddVehicleView.save()` now calls `noteEntryChanged()` so the add path raises a signal at all. Verified by the orchestrator: swiftlint 0 errors, `swift build` 0, `GarageUITests` **7 tests 0 failures**, and mutation-checked personally - removing the add-path signal fails the regression test. `SCREENMAP.md` gained a "tab roots reload on signals, not on reappearance" convention, which is the durable part: the tab roots stay mounted across a switch, so `.task` fires once and never again |

The screen is therefore telling the truth - `IN RUB ≈ –  converts when online`, `1 entry pending rates`, `SEPTEMBER SPEND 0 ₽` - and will keep telling it forever. **This makes RV.20 ACTIVE rather than latent**: `date_req` support landed today and is verified working for a past date, and this is the backfill that row said was "a question of when, not if" | A backfill that walks the missing dates and fetches each - CBR `?date_req=dd/MM/yyyy` and NBK `?fdate=dd.MM.yyyy`, both verified against 15.08.2026. **Bound it and say what you chose**: the client only ever asks for 400 days, so that is the natural ceiling, but 400 dates x 2 feeds is 800 requests and needs to be paced, idempotent (the insert is already `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) and restartable - a backfill that re-runs from zero on every deploy is its own outage. Decide too whether it runs once or continuously: gaps will recur whenever a feed is down for a day, and carry-forward only covers a gap **after** a first publication exists. L2: with history present for a quote and a gap in the middle, the backfill fills exactly the gap and nothing else; with no history at all, it establishes the window. **Then verify the reported entry actually converts** - a green test with that 2 Sep fill still pending means the chain is not closed  **SHAPE DECIDED 2026-09-03 (product owner): the backfill is DEMAND-DRIVEN, not a fixed window.** The trigger is *"we hold an entry whose `rateDate` has no rate"*, which covers the case that made a fixed window wrong: **import**. `POST /import/parse` lets a user bring in years of MFM history in one action (`docs/API.md`, the rule-9 exception), and those entries can be dated well outside any rolling window - a 400-day ceiling would leave everything older permanently pending, which is the bug this row exists for, reintroduced one day later. So: **the set of dates to fetch is derived from the data, not from the calendar.** Consequences to design for. **(a) The device knows which dates are missing; the server does not** - it holds no entries (hard rule 9), so the *device* must ask, either by requesting a rate for a date it lacks or by widening its pack request. Do not invent a server-side domain query to find them; that is exactly what rule 9 forbids. **(b) An import can request hundreds of distinct dates at once** - bound the burst, dedupe, and make it resumable; a user importing five years must not fire 1800 upstream requests. **(c) The upstream has no rate for a weekend or a holiday**, and asking again every launch would be a permanent retry loop - a date answered "nothing published" must be recorded as answered, distinct from "not yet asked". **(d) Backfilled rows are `published` when they come from a real document for that date and `:carried-forward` only when they do not** - the distinction RV.20 established.  **GAP RULE DECIDED 2026-09-03 (product owner): a date with no published rate takes the most recent earlier known rate, within a bounded window.** This is the right shape and it dissolves the "answered-empty vs not-yet-asked" bookkeeping entirely - a weekend resolves from Friday and is finished, so nothing retries. Look **backwards only**: the rate in force on the day of the fill is one that already existed; a later rate did not. The row is stored `:carried-forward`, never `published` (RV.20). **The window was proposed as 7 days and MEASURED AGAINST THE LIVE FEED as too short.** CBR does not publish across the Russian New Year: asking `date_req` for 01, 05, 08, 09, 10 and 12 January 2026 all return the document dated **31.12.2025**, and the first January document is **13.01.2026** - a **13-day gap**. A 7-day window would leave 07-12 January permanently unresolved, in **RUB**, which is a home currency for this app. **Use 14 days** (13 measured, one day of margin) and pin the January window as a test fixture - the holiday document is already captured at `backend/tests/.../Fixtures/cbr-xml-daily-2026-01-04-holiday-windows1251.xml`. **The bound is not decoration**: without it an imported entry predating all rate history would silently take some unrelated rate rather than staying honestly pending. **And carrying back must not hide a broken feed** - RV.15 went unnoticed for weeks because nothing downstream looked wrong; `SourcesFailed` must still be reported even while gaps are being filled. |

| **[x]** RV.36 | **A carried-forward placeholder permanently displaces the real published rate for that day.** Found while resolving RV.30. `RateRepository.UpsertAsync` is `ON CONFLICT (date, base, quote) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL DO NOTHING` - the append-only guarantee. The 2026-09-03 data is the proof - **29 ECB quotes for that date are all `ecb:carried-forward`**, i.e. 09-02's rates standing in for 09-03, and no later pass can correct them. L2: a carried row is replaced by a later published row for the same date/base/quote; a published row is **not** replaced by another published row; a carried row is not replaced by another carried row. The append-only guarantee for real data is untouched: published never overwrites published, carried never overwrites carried. `RateSources` gained the suffix as the single source of truth rather than a literal at the call site. Verified by the orchestrator: `dotnet build` 0, `dotnet format` 0, **339 tests 0 skipped** (336 before), and mutation-checked personally - restoring the blanket `DO NOTHING` fails **1 of 3** tests, the supersede case, while **both negatives still pass**, which is the discrimination that proves they guard the append-only rule rather than the new behaviour. No stored `money` value is rewritten (hard rule 3: snapshots immutable) - an entry already converted at the carried rate keeps it |

| **[x]** RV.105 | **`POST /sync/push` costs 578 ms PER RECORD server-side, and it is four seconds from re-entering [RV.97]'s livelock.** Production log, 2026-09-07 05:08-05:17 (device `787c4f6f`, build `1.0.0+806`, the owner's 513-row MFM import): three pushes, **69 records / 39 841 ms**, **93 records / 53 688 ms**, **92 records / 53 140 ms** - all `Accepted=…, Conflicts=0, Rejected=0`. That is **577, 577 and 578 ms per record**, linear to three significant figures, so it is a **fixed per-record cost**, not payload size and not CPU. Pulls in the same minutes return 69 records in **1 021 ms**, so push is ~50x more expensive per record than pull. **The cause is one transaction per record**: `SyncRepository.ApplyChangeAsync` opens a transaction, does `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`, allocates an SCN, writes, and commits - **once for every change in the batch** (`SyncRepository.cs:125-137`), while `SyncService` loops the changes one at a time (`SyncService.cs:70,100`). A 92-record batch is therefore 92 serialized commits, and ~578 ms is the fsync-and-round-trip cost of one commit on this host. **The four-second margin, calculated not guessed**: [RV.97] gave the client a 120 s upload budget and capped a batch at 64 KB **or 200 records, whichever binds first**. These fills are large enough that 64 KB binds at ~90 records - but a batch of smaller records (a delete cascade, reminders, preferences) binds on the COUNT, and **200 x 578 ms = 115.6 s against a 120 s budget**. One slower disk day, or one more index, and the client abandons a push the server is still committing - which is exactly [RV.97], at a bigger number. **[RV.97] is confirmed working in the same log** (bounded batches, every one accepted, no `499`); this row is the cost it exposed | **Amortize the commits without losing per-record outcomes - that tension IS the row.** One transaction for the whole batch is the obvious fix and it breaks a stated contract: `docs/SYNC.md:668` promises **partial batch acceptance** (idempotent by id + `baseScn`), so a single conflicting record must not roll back the other 91. **And the per-record transaction is load-bearing for a second reason the code states**: the class comment says SCNs are allocated inside the row's own transaction so that "committed SCNs are contiguous and commit in order, which is what lets a pull cursor page the stream without ever skipping an in-flight commit" - a redesign must keep that or the pull cursor can skip a record forever. Shapes worth measuring, in order: **one transaction per batch with per-record outcomes collected inside it** (conflicts become no-ops within the transaction rather than rollbacks); **a batched SCN allocation** (one range for the batch instead of N allocations); a **multi-row upsert** for the accepted set once conflicts are known. **Measure, do not assume**: report ms per record before and after on a batch of 92, and say which change bought what. **Do not** solve it by lowering [RV.97]'s record cap - that hides the cost behind more round trips and leaves the per-record price untouched | L2 (the row's point): a push of **200 records** - the maximum a client may legally send - completes **well inside** the 120 s client budget, with the measured ms/record reported before and after. L1/L2: a batch containing one conflicting record still returns `accepted` for the others and `conflict` for that one (partial acceptance, `SYNC.md:668`). L2: SCNs assigned by a batch remain contiguous and in commit order, and a pull cursor paging the stream during concurrent pushes never skips a record. **Vacuous traps**: timing a batch of 5 records, where 578 ms x 5 is invisible; asserting the total duration fell without reporting **ms per record**, which is the number that predicts the 200-record case; making the batch atomic and calling partial acceptance an acceptable loss without a product-owner decision written here; testing on a laptop SSD and concluding the fsync cost is gone - the production host is the measurement that matters |

L2: SCNs assigned to a batch are contiguous and strictly increasing, and match `AssignedScnRange`. L2: a rejected record still rejects without rolling the batch back. **Vacuous traps**: measuring on a local Postgres and concluding it is fast; batching the write but leaving the per-record SELECT or the per-record SCN allocation, which is two thirds of the cost; breaking partial acceptance to get one statement; asserting elapsed time rather than command count |

| **[x]** RV.97 | **Every sync push after a large import dies at the CLIENT'S OWN 30-second timeout, and the cycle relives it forever.** Production log, 2026-09-06 20:05-20:07 (device `787c4f6f`, build `1.0.0+788`): five consecutive `POST /v1/sync/push` requests of **145-151 KB** end **499** (client gone) after **31.8 s, 31.1 s, 13.5 s, 31.9 s and 4.1 s**, while every `GET /v1/sync/pull` in the same minute succeeds in **490-750 ms**. **The 30 s clustering is the tell, and the code names it**: `TransportTimeouts.readJSON = 30` is the idle/read budget for "ordinary JSON calls (sign-in, **sync**, rates, gateway, catalog, config)", while `upload = 120` exists because "blob PUT and import multipart carry megabytes over a mobile uplink". **Sync push carries megabytes too after an import and never asks for the upload budget** - `RemoteSyncTransport` sets no per-request `timeoutInterval`, so a 150 KB body on a mobile uplink runs out of read budget mid-flight. **The server does the work anyway** (31 s of it) and throws the answer away, which is what `499` means. **And the cycle cannot escape**: the batch is `batchLimit: Int = 200` **records**, never bytes, so the same oversized batch is rebuilt and retried every cycle - the pull SCN marches 283 -> 322 -> 372 -> 422 -> 442 -> 492 while the dirty queue never drains. A user who imports their history has a device that pushes 150 KB, fails, and repeats - on their battery and their data. **This is the shape [RV.65] warned about from the other side** (a doubled 53 KB upload visible only because the server logged it) | **Two independent defects; fix both and say which fixed what.** (1) **The budget**: sync push must ask for the upload budget the way blob PUT and import already do - a per-request `timeoutInterval`, not a new global default, because raising `readJSON` would slow every failing GET. (2) **The batch**: `batchLimit` counts RECORDS, so 200 attachments-worth of payload is unbounded in bytes - **bound the batch by encoded size** as well as by count, and say what the limit is and why. **The loop is now MEASURED, not hypothesised** (second log, 20:05-20:22, seventeen minutes): the write is `INSERT`-or-`UPDATE` keyed on `(account_id, id)` (`SyncRepository.cs:51-65`), so a retry does **not** duplicate rows - but it **assigns a new SCN every time**, and that closes the cycle. Push (31 s of real server work) -> client abandons at its 30 s budget and never learns the outcome -> **rows stay dirty locally** -> pull returns the device its OWN just-written rows, fifty at a time (`SinceScn` 492 -> 546 -> 596 on a single-device account, where nothing else can be writing) -> still dirty -> the same 150 KB goes again. Every retry also rewrites the whole account and would wake any OTHER device to re-download everything, forever. Either way the answer belongs in `docs/SYNC.md`. **Do not** simply raise the timeout and leave the batch unbounded - that trades a 30 s failure for a 120 s one on a bigger import | L1: a push batch whose encoded size exceeds the cap splits into several requests, each under it, and every record still lands exactly once. L1: the sync push request carries the upload timeout, not the JSON one (assert the request, not the constant). L2: a push of the owner's import volume completes against the real endpoint. **Vacuous traps**: testing with a handful of small records, where neither limit binds; asserting `batchLimit` changed rather than the encoded SIZE of what goes on the wire; raising the timeout and calling it fixed without a size bound - the next import is bigger |

Fifty is exactly `BackfillBatchSize`, so a full batch is drained every pass and **nothing is published, nothing carried, nothing even fails** - and it continues across long windows with no device traffic at all (06:56-07:31 has no sync, no pack, nothing). **`SettleAsync` is called unconditionally** on every date in the batch (`RateBackfillService.cs:101-104`) and deletes by `(base, date)`, so rows ARE being removed - which means they are being **put back**. **Prime suspect, verified as a predicate mismatch in the code**: `RecordRequestAsync` enqueues a date when `!sources.IsSubsetOf(families)` - i.e. when **any** feed family lacks a row - while `BackfillDatesAsync` skips a feed when `families.Contains(feed.Source)` - i.e. it only fetches from feeds that have **no** row. The user's answer lives in `ImportFlowModel.effectiveCurrency` and is passed to `ImportConversion` for the ENTRIES, but never to the Vehicle the import creates - so the rows land as KZT against a EUR home. **The consequence is not cosmetic**: every imported entry is then a foreign-currency entry needing a KZT->EUR rate per its own date, so a clean import turns into a log full of rate-pending rows ([RV.140], [RV.152]) for a user who never asked for a second currency. Hard rule 13 - a value the user stated is theirs - is violated at the moment of statement. **(2) The name**: `ImportFlowModel.newCarName` (`:323-327`) derives it from the file's `Vehicle name` column, else `pickedFormat?.displayName` - which is literally **"Drivvo"** (`ImportFormats.cs`) - else a localized fallback, and the mapping gate offers no field to change it. Hard rule 13 requires a derived value be editable **at the moment it is offered** and again afterwards; only the second half holds (the Garage can rename it) | **Pass the declared currency into the new car, and let the user name it at the gate.** `newCar(named:)` takes the currency rather than assuming one - it is a parameter, not a default - and the mapping step gains an editable name field pre-filled with today's derived value, so the derivation stays a suggestion. **Check the units too while you are there**: the same factory hardcodes km/L/L-per-100 and `paceLimitKmPerDay: 1500`, and a KZT importer is unlikely to be the only one who wanted something else - report whether the import knows better, do not guess. **Do not touch the ENTRY conversion**, which already honours the declared currency | L1: a new car created by an import carries the DECLARED currency - assert the stored `homeCurrency`, with a fixture whose declared currency is not EUR, or the assertion passes on the defect. L1: its entries and the car agree, so no imported row is rate-pending purely because of the car's home. L4: the gate offers an editable name pre-filled with the derived value, and a typed name reaches the saved car. L1: an import into an EXISTING car still leaves that car's home currency untouched ([RV.152]'s rule - a pick must not re-home a car the user already owns). **Vacuous traps**: a fixture declaring EUR, where the hardcode and the fix are indistinguishable; asserting the name field exists rather than that a typed name is saved; fixing the car and leaving the entries, or the reverse; re-homing an existing destination car, which is a different decision | **DONE 2026-09-10, and it took a second pass.** `TargetCar.newCar` now REQUIRES a `homeCurrency` - no call site can silently reach EUR again - and the import passes the file's declared currency or the user's answer. The name is editable where the car is offered: the preview's "Imports into" card on the single-car path and the chosen lane's card on the multi-car gate, pre-filled and typed over, the id preserved so classified fills keep their destination. **Three defects the orchestrator found in the agent's work, all fixed before the commit**: (1) `swiftlint` exited **2**, not 0 as reported - `HomeUITests.swift` was two lines over the file-length ceiling; (2) **both screenshots showed no name field at all** - the preview's card sits below the fold and the mapping gate's field was on the SECOND lane, whose card starts at the frame edge, so a `-importScrollToTargetCar` hook and a first-lane seed were added and the frames re-shot; (3) the pre-fill was **`"Drivvo"`**, the format's display name - which is *the exact thing the owner complained about* (*"I got default - drivvo"*), so `pickedFormat?.displayName` is out of the chain and the fallback is the localized `Imported car`. The field also had **no resting affordance** - a `TextField` styled as plain text among static labels - so `editableValueUnderline` draws a line at rest and accents it while typing (hard rule 13 offers a value the user cannot see is editable only nominally). Screenshots EN+RU, opened and checked.

L1: a date-only value never renders a time component. L4: EN and RU. **Vacuous traps**: a fixture where the scan and the receipt date are the same day, which passes either way; asserting the string contains a date; fixing the label and leaving the `.hour().minute()` formatter on a date-only field | **DONE 2026-09-10.** The caption was built from the receipt's PRINTED date, which is date-only, then formatted with a time - so every scan read `00:00`, a precision the value never had. It now reads the attachment's `createdAt`, the real capture instant ("Captured Sep 10 at 2:32 PM" / "Снято 10 сент., 14:32"), and the printed date renders as a date with no time beside it. The rule is recorded in `docs/DESIGN.md` -> Typography: **format at the precision the value has.** **Mutation**: pointing the caption back at `extractedTimestamp` turns the L1 red on "the caption must be built from Attachment.createdAt"; restored, 3 pass. Screenshots EN+RU, opened by the orchestrator - both show the capture instant and the printed date as separate, correctly-formatted values.

Both builders now carry it, and `ScannedSaveValues` gained `stationName` so the scan path's `userCorrected` stays honest. | **[x]** RV.176 | **Some committed screenshots cannot be reproduced by the capture script.** Found by the [PJ.55] agent, outside its fence: `design/screenshots/RV.150-station*.png` are committed, but `scripts/capture-screenshots.sh` had **no `stationSettings` pose at all** until PJ.55 added one - so those frames were captured out-of-band and **no line in the script can re-shoot them**. Nothing was deleted, so no doc or site reference broke. **Mutation**: deleting the `RV.150-station` line with its PNG committed makes the check exit 1 naming the file, exactly reproducing RV.150's own state; restored, exit 0 on 470 frames. 14 check-script test cases pass. **The `legacy` section was the agent's own call and PR.28 did not anticipate it**: the accent-tabbar frame is a hard-rule-5 record `SITE.md` cites and `simctl` cannot re-stage a springboard or a system banner.

The vocabulary is **bilingual** and lives with the other extraction vocabularies; `expense.category.suggest` records the category CODE and whether the user changed it - shape only, never the receipt's text (hard rule 12), the same `userCorrected` signal `capture.pipeline` already keeps for fuel. **Mutation (orchestrator-run)**: making `infer` always return nil turns the parking and the Russian L1s red; restored byte-identical. Screenshots EN+RU opened - the RU scan lands on **КАТЕГОРИЯ · Парковка** with its chevron. **The finding that matters most is what could NOT be measured**: the receipts corpus holds **16 images and zero non-fuel ones**, so the vocabulary is verified against hand-written text fixtures rather than photographs. | **[x]** RV.155 | (J11 new phone - the pull that restores it) **The pull cursor went BACKWARDS and re-fetched 274 records it already had.** Production logs 2026-09-09, same device and session: `06:22:26 SinceScn=1389 -> NextSince=1405`, `06:24:00 SinceScn=1405 -> NextSince=1589`, then **`06:28:45 SinceScn=1405`** again, returning **274** rows and advancing to 1679. L3: a pull whose cycle is interrupted after the pull but before the push still advances the cursor. Reconstructed step by step against the 2026-09-09 log. **Fix**: `SeededSyncCursorStore` - `load` answers the seed so a restore still pulls from 0, `save` writes through to the durable store on every advance; the end-of-cycle save is deleted; `SYNC.md` says when an advance becomes durable. **Mutation (orchestrator-run)**: dropping the write-through turns `overlappingCyclesShareTheCursorAdvance` red - *"the second overlapping cycle resumes from the first's nextSince"* (`since 0` where `10` was expected); restored byte-identical, 3/3. | **[x]** RV.158 | (F9 currency rate unavailable for that date) **A rate-span drain fires eight `/v1/rates/pack` requests in seven milliseconds, and seven come back empty.** Device log, 2026-09-09 08:56:17 on `1.0.0+925`: eight `net.request endpoint=/v1/rates/pack` lines between `.104` and `.111`, each answered `status=200` in **0-1 ms** with **`responseBytes=63`** - an empty pack - and a ninth at `.111` answered `1637 ms` with `67285` bytes. This is `RateStore.fetchSpan` (`RateStore.swift:247-274`) walking a multi-year span in `packWindowDays = 400` chunks with **no pacing and no early exit**: it loops `while cursor <= last`, and an empty answer is not distinguishable from a served one, so a span reaching back years costs one request per 400 days whether or not the server has anything for those dates. **The empty answers are the more important half**: the owner's rate-pending rows are dated in ranges the server answers with nothing, so the drain that exists to resolve them cannot, no matter how often it runs. That is a DATA-COVERAGE question ([RV.135]'s historical feed), not a client bug, and this row must not paper over it | **Two things, and keep them separate.** (1) **Client**: pace or bound the chunk walk - a burst of eight requests inside one millisecond is indistinguishable from a retry storm at the origin and will trip rate limiting the moment the server has any. Consider stopping early when a chunk answers empty AND the span is older than the feed's known coverage, but **do not stop on the first empty chunk alone** - a gap in the middle of a covered range is legitimate. (2) **Server/data**: establish what date range `/v1/rates/pack` actually covers, per base and quote, and record it. If the coverage cannot reach an imported multi-year history, say so in `docs/SCHEMA.md` -> Exchange rates, because it changes what the F9 footnote can honestly promise and what [RV.152]'s 'convert the log' answer can deliver. **A logging gap this exposed**: `net.request` records the endpoint but not the requested span, so which ranges are empty cannot be read from a log. A span expressed in DAYS (a duration, not dates) is shape, not a domain value - add it | L1: a span covering N chunk-widths issues exactly the expected number of requests, and they are paced rather than simultaneous. L1: an empty chunk in the middle of a covered span does not abort the walk. L2: the server reports its actual coverage, and a request outside it is distinguishable from a request inside it that happens to be empty. **Vacuous traps**: stopping on the first empty chunk, which silently truncates a legitimate gap; asserting the request count without asserting they are not simultaneous; treating the empty answers as a client bug and 'fixing' the client so the user never learns the rates do not exist **Shipped 2026-09-12** (flash, one dispatch with [RV.138]). **Fix**: `/v1/rates/pack` carries an additive `coverageFloor` (the oldest date any feed can serve for the base; ECB states 1999-01-04, CIS/NBK state none) and `RateStore.fetchSpan` reads it - a chunk entirely below a stated floor is skipped to the floor instead of costing a request per 400 days, an empty chunk INSIDE coverage does not abort the walk, chunks are paced, and one in-flight span per base dedupes a concurrent caller. `API.md` documents the field as additive; `SCHEMA.md` -> Exchange rates states the coverage. **Mutation (orchestrator-run)**: disabling the floor skip turns `fetchSpanStopsAtTheServerCoverageFloor` red (`ranges.dropFirst().allSatisfy { $0.to >= floor }`), 1 of 23 in `RateStoreTests`; restored, 23/23. Gates in the orchestrator's hands: `swift test` 0 at **2044 tests / 247 suites**, `TankbookTests` 0 at **196**, lint 0. **Not shipped here**: the `net.request` span-in-days log field - it touches the transport's request metadata, a separate decision; filed as [RV.254]. |

| **[x]** RV.138 | (F9 the rate carry-forward's horizon) **The daily carry-forward has no horizon, and [RV.135] just made that matter.** Found by the RV.135 agent and confirmed: `RatesJobService.CarryForwardAsync` (`:104-125`) orders each quote's published rows by date and walks `for (var day = published[0].Date; day <= today; ...)`, inserting a carried-forward row for **every** gap day. `published[0]` is the **OLDEST** published row, so the walk starts wherever history begins. That is roughly **4,000 dates x ~30 quotes** on one pass. The carry itself is stepwise and NOT a 2015 rate smeared forward (the loop advances its `lastRate` as it passes each published row), so this is a **volume and occupancy** problem, not a wrong-number one: the inserted rows then occupy dates that genuine demand would otherwise backfill with a real published value, and [RV.36]'s supersede only fires for a date someone actually asks for | **Bound the walk.** The demand backfill already has `CarryBackWindowDays = 14` and the daily carry-forward has nothing - decide its horizon and write it into `docs/SCHEMA.md` -> Exchange rates beside the existing one. The obvious shape is to start the walk at `today - N` rather than at the oldest row, since carrying a decade of gap days serves no device: a device asking for an old date gets it through the demand path, which fetches the real value. **Do not** solve it by making the demand backfill store less - the deep rows are the point of [RV.135] | L2: a base whose oldest published row is a decade old inserts a **bounded** number of carried rows on one daily pass - assert the count, not that it "completes". L2: a gap day inside the horizon still carries forward exactly as today. L2: a decade-old published row is **not** deleted or altered by the pass. **Vacuous traps**: asserting the job runs; testing with a fresh database whose oldest row is days old, where the defect cannot appear; bounding the insert count by batching rather than by a horizon, which keeps the same rows arriving over more passes **Shipped 2026-09-12** (flash, with [RV.158]). **Fix**: `RateOptions.CarryForwardHorizonDays` (default 400, the device's own pack window); `CarryForwardAsync` walks from `today - (horizon - 1)` or the oldest published row when that is more recent, seeding the carried rate from the most recent published row at or before the start, so a 2015 backfill no longer makes the next daily pass insert a decade of rows. Idempotent across passes. **Mutation (orchestrator-run)**: walking from `published[0].Date` again turns `CarryForward_RespectsTheHorizon_SoADecadeOldAnchorInsertsABoundedNumber` red (*expected a bounded 1..60 carried rows, got 8500*); restored, 7/7. Gates in the orchestrator's hands: `dotnet build` 0, `dotnet format` 0, `dotnet test` 0 at **457**. |
| **[x]** RV.134 | (J3b type it - the unit in the sentence) **Five more places bake a unit into the sentence, and two are on the screen [RV.126] just fixed.** Found 2026-09-08: three by the RV.126 agent's sibling audit, **two more by opening its own RU screenshot** - the check no test performs. On a miles/gallons car the Confirm screen renders the odometer in `миль` and the volume field in `гал`, then labels the price row **`Цена / л`** (`ManualFillUpSections.swift:147`, key `"Price / L"`) and captions it **`считается из суммы ÷ литров`** (`:150`, `"fills in from total ÷ liters"`). So one screen states the user's units correctly in two rows and contradicts itself in two others. The audit missed them because [RV.126] scoped it to `ManualFillUpFormState.swift`; the screenshot did not. The other three: `ManualFillUpSections.swift:389` hardcodes `"+\(delta.km) km since last"` for **every** car, beside a field whose own label is per-vehicle; `ServiceEntryFormState.swift:184` and `ImportReviewRowFields.swift:53` hardcode the km key the same way. **This is hard rule 10 and hard rule 13 together**: a value the user set is stated back to them wrong | **One full localised sentence per unit, exactly as [RV.126] did it** - `OdometerConflict.quote(day:odometer:distanceUnit:)` is the pattern to copy, and its reason is the one that matters: RU declines the units differently (`км` indeclinable, `миль` genitive plural, `л`/`гал`), so a unit token spliced into a shared stem is wrong in Russian however right it looks in English. **Do the volume pair too** - `Price / L` and the `÷ liters` caption need the litre/gallon split, not just the distance ones. **Then add the guard that would have caught all five**: a test that scans the catalogue for keys whose text contains a bare unit token, against an explicit allowlist of the unit LABELS themselves (`"km"`, `"mi"`, `"L"`, `"gal"`). An allowlist of every offending key is the vacuous version and guards nothing | L1: each fixed sentence renders correctly for both units in EN and RU, asserted as exact strings including the RU plural. L1: the catalogue guard **fails** when a new unit-spliced key is added - prove it by adding one, not by asserting the guard exists. L4: a miles/gallons car's Confirm screen names miles and gallons in **every** row, with no `km`, `км`, `л` or `L` anywhere. **Vacuous traps**: fixing the distance sites and leaving `Price / L`, which is half the screen; splicing `L10n.volumeUnit(...)` into the translated stem, which is the same bug in a new place; an allowlist that names the offenders rather than the unit labels; testing only EN, where the declension problem is invisible | **DONE 2026-09-11.** `ManualFillUpUnitCopy` - one full localised sentence per (sentence, unit): *Price / L* / *Price / gal*, the `total ÷ liters` / `÷ gallons` caption, the `km`/`mi since last` delta, and the two km keys in `ServiceEntryFormState` and `ImportReviewRowFields`. **The seam audit is the deliverable**: 61 catalogue keys matched; the per-unit ones are listed as correct, and **seven more classes of the same defect are listed as found and not fixed** - filed as [RV.234]. **Mutation (orchestrator-run)**: restoring *Price / L* on the gallon path turns `RV134UnitCopyTests` red; restored byte-identical, 6/6. Gates in the orchestrator's hands: `swift test` 0 at **2016 tests / 244 suites**, lint 0. Screenshots EN+RU opened on an imperial car: *Price / gal* and *Цена / гал* with the gallons caption - **and the same frame shows the volume row still labelled *Liters* / *Литры* over a `gal` field, and the disabled-save hint still says *liters*.** Two of RV.234's seven, visible in this row's own picture. |
| **[x]** RV.108 | (J11a first sign-in - the account endpoint) **`GET /v1/account` is normative in `docs/API.md` and does not exist.** From `docs/BACKEND-TEST-REVIEW.md` and **verified in the source**: `docs/API.md:536` documents `GET /account` -> `{ accountId, email, createdAt, storage: { usedBytes, quota }, llm: { used, quota, period } }`, and `Program.cs:598-603` maps only `account.MapGet("/devices")`, `MapPut("/devices/{id}/push-token")`, `MapDelete("/devices/{id}")` and `MapDelete("")` - **there is no bare `MapGet` on the account group**, and no test references the route. A contract with neither a route nor a test is a promise to a client that would 404 | **Resolve the mismatch in ONE direction and say which**: implement the documented summary with its tests, or delete the row from `API.md` because nothing consumes it. **Check the client first** - if the iOS Settings or Account & devices screen renders storage or quota figures, find where they come from today; if they come from nowhere, that is a second finding and the endpoint is wanted. `API.md` is the authority for the HTTP contract and a change there is a breaking-change review either way | L2 if implemented: the summary returns the documented shape for the authenticated account, 401 without a bearer, and never another account's figures. If removed: the doc no longer promises it, and a grep for the route in client and tests comes back empty. **Vacuous traps**: implementing it because it is written down without checking whether any client wants it; deleting the line without checking the same | **DONE 2026-09-12 - decided in one direction: the row is deleted from `API.md`.** The client was checked first, as the brief demanded: `AccountClient` implements exactly three account calls and no summary read; `accountId`/`email` arrive with the session; `createdAt`, storage and LLM usage render on no screen; the Settings quota card is a blob-429 error state fed only by a DEBUG fixture. A contract with neither a route, a test nor a reader is a promise a client would 404 on - so the promise goes, not the code. `BACKEND-TEST-REVIEW.md` finding resolved; one gateway comment reworded. `dotnet build` 0, `dotnet format` 0, `gate.sh` 0 (2030 package / 195 app-target tests). Grep proof in the report: no `GET /account` remains anywhere. **Found and filed**: [RV.253]. |

## OB · Observability **[v1.0.x]** - the device can say what it did

**Defined 2026-09-05 (product owner).** Not a new backlog: this groups four rows that already
existed as `PR.9`, `PR.10`, `PR.11` and `PR.12` and names why they belong together. **The evidence
for the grouping is this week's bug hunt.** RV.58, RV.59, RV.60, RV.65 and RV.66 were all diagnosed
from **server** logs, because the device emits almost nothing - and two of them needed the
orchestrator to read client source to guess what the app had done. One of those guesses (RV.58's
cross-account claim) was **wrong and had to be retracted**, precisely because no client-side record
existed to check it against.

The cluster's value is not a feature. It is that the next production report should be answerable
from the device's own account of itself, instead of inferred from what the server happened to see.
**SH.3, the human floor walk, will generate exactly that kind of question**, which is the argument
for landing at least `OB.2` before it.

Build order, and it is a real dependency chain, not a preference:

| Order | Row | Was | Why here |
|---|---|---|---|
| **OB.1** | Error codes on the wire | `PR.9` | A stable `code` is what every later layer maps from; a status alone cannot name a next step (hard rule 7). RV.65's new sign-in message is the first consumer. |
| **OB.2** | Emit the defined events and async edges | `PR.10` | The foundation - nothing can be exported or persisted that was never emitted. Highest value alone: `NetRequest/Response` would have shown RV.59's duplicate config fetch and RV.65's doubled 53 KB upload from the client side. |
| **OB.3** | Persist last sync date and last failure | `PR.12` | Needs OB.1's `code` to record a failure meaningfully. The natural sibling of RV.58's revoked-device card - the state survives a relaunch instead of vanishing. |
| **OB.4** | Diagnostics export end to end | `PR.11` | Needs OB.2's events to have anything worth exporting; it is the user-facing delivery of the other three. |

The four rows keep their `PR.*` ids and their own text - **this section is the ordering and the
rationale, not a duplicate backlog.** Marking any of them done marks the OB step done.

## Working rules

- One task = one PR = code + its checks; the PR description quotes the task ID and its check list, ticked.
- A task discovering an undocumented behavior extends the owning doc in the same PR (CLAUDE.md rule).
- Snapshot baselines are reviewed like code; accuracy high-water marks only ratchet up.
